2026-06-24T03:10:40+08:00 Began Workbench item 3 rigid branch enforcement by implementing the missing SAM2/depth-to-visible-geometry bridge. Added `scripts/build_v19_visible_geometry_from_sam2_depth.py`, a measurement adapter that consumes a raw frame manifest, SAM2 per-track masks, metric depth NPZ, and camera poses/base annotations; it outputs `annotations_v19_visible_geometry.json`, `v19_visible_geometry_adapter_report.json`, and `v19_visible_geometry_depth_fused_report.json`. Physical mechanism: mask pixels + metric depth + intrinsics + camera pose are back-projected into finite world surfels; the script writes V18-compatible `visible_geometry_candidate.world_vertices_sample_m` and centroid-initialized `reconstructed_geometry_pose` fields for downstream rigid completion/pose tools. It is explicitly not final object pose. Failure semantics: without a real camera/world pose it fails unless `--allow-camera-frame-world` is explicit, preventing a silent coordinate-frame fallback. Verification: `python -m py_compile scripts/build_v19_visible_geometry_from_sam2_depth.py` passed; `--help` exposed the intended contract; a single synthetic smoke fixture with two masks, depth rows, and camera poses produced 2 visible metric frames, 30 surfels on frame 0, and a translated frame-1 centroid, confirming the backprojection/pose-prior mechanism. Updated `docs/v19_english_orchestration.md` to route Section 7 through the new adapter and Section 9 rigid branch commands through the adapter outputs; updated `docs/v19_component_extraction.md` and EPISTEMIC.md so the SAM2/depth bridge is no longer listed as missing. Remaining blockers: standalone fresh raw-frame manifest, full hand/object/camera base-state builder, V19 state-to-render adapter, and benchmark adapters. Next action: Workbench item 4 representative rigid-branch run/iteration using real project measurements.

2026-06-24T18:05:00+08:00 Investigated user question: source of truth for focal/intrinsics and whether object/hand paths use different intrinsics. Observation: the raw frame manifest records source/manifest image sizes only, no calibration. The task sidecar JSON is action metadata only, no camera intrinsics. V19 HaWoR QC records `img_focal=600.0` in `/data2/ego_annotation_outputs/v19_runs/20260624_task5_fresh_v19_pipeline_v0/measurements/hand_candidates/hawor_world/qc_hawor_world_hands.json`; V18 HaWoR QC records `img_focal=2304.0`. V19 UniDepth full-frame archive records per-frame `intrinsics_fx_fy_cx_cy` with median focal ~1083 px and frame-720 `[1066.3185, 1075.9944, 967.8867, 526.5381]`. V19 base annotations for frame 720 use camera pose source `hawor_npz_R_c2w_t_c2w` but intrinsics source `depth_npz_intrinsics_fx_fy_cx_cy`. Code evidence: `build_v19_base_annotations.py` prioritizes depth NPZ intrinsics over camera NPZ and HaWoR focal; `build_v19_visible_geometry_from_sam2_depth.py` back-projects object mask/depth pixels using depth NPZ intrinsics and then transforms them with the base camera pose. Therefore the current V19 run has no single calibration source of truth and silently mixes HaWoR-600 hand/world export with UniDepth-~1083 object/depth backprojection. The 2D detectors/masks are image-space; the inconsistency enters at metric lifting/world export, not at box/mask detection itself. `2304` is a V18/remote-script contract (`scripts/remote_run_hawor_export.sh` default), not yet proven dataset ground-truth calibration for V19.

2026-06-24T18:13:00+08:00 Follow-up focal provenance audit. Observation: V19 DROID QC states `calibration_source: dataset has no calibration file; pinhole prior uses focal_scale * max(width, height)` and records `focal_scale=1.2`, source width 1920, hence `fx=fy=2304`. `scripts/run_droid_full_frame.py` implements `focal = focal_scale * max(info.width, info.height)` with default `--focal-scale 1.2`. `scripts/remote_run_hawor_export.sh` independently defaults `EGO_HAWOR_IMG_FOCAL` to 2304 and passes it as `--img_focal`; V18 HaWoR QC therefore records 2304. The V19 fresh launch `/tmp/launch_v19_fresh_measurements.sh` called `scripts/export_hawor_world.py` without `--img_focal`, so HaWoR returned/wrote `img_focal=600`. UniDepth focal ~1083 comes from `scripts/run_unidepth_full_frame_v3.py`: it calls `infer_unidepth`, takes the returned intrinsics matrix, rescales to source size, and stores `intrinsics_fx_fy_cx_cy`; frame-720 was `[1066.3185, 1075.9944, 967.8867, 526.5381]`. Physical implication: for a 1920x1080 frame, focal 2304 implies ~45.2° horizontal FOV, 1083 implies ~83.1°, and 600 implies ~116.0°. These cannot all be the same camera calibration. Current claim: 2304 is a prior/contract from DROID/V18, 1083 is a UniDepth-estimated per-frame intrinsics hypothesis, and 600 is a HaWoR default/omitted-argument result; none is yet proven dataset ground-truth calibration.

Focal MAD is 13.29 px (~1.23%). All-frame p05-p95 focal is 994.5-1136.7 (~13.1% span), driven by outliers/scene changes, especially early frames around 800-860 and frame 937 high at 1311.7. The task-critical contact interval 690-725 is much tighter: median focal `1076.98`, p05-p95 `1064.43-1091.24`. 2026-06-25T11:52:46+08:00 Started HOT3D metric-camera adapter work after completing the 2D box slice. Documentation/equation evidence: HOT3D clips README states `vis_clips.py --undistort` warps original fisheye cameras to pinhole cameras; HOT3D `clip_util.convert_to_pinhole_camera` constructs `PinholePlaneCameraModel` with the source camera's `f` and `c`; `hand_tracking_toolkit.camera.from_json` confirms Aria `CameraModelType.FISHEYE624` projection_params order as `[f, cx, cy, 12 distortion coeffs]` with `fx=fy=f`; `hand_tracking_toolkit.dataset.warp_image` maps destination pinhole pixels to source fisheye pixels through camera rays. Local environment lacks `hand_tracking_toolkit` and `projectaria_tools`, so implemented a minimal dependency-free port of the documented projection/distortion equations in `scripts/build_v19_hot3d_pinhole_adapter.py`. Scope: sensor camera adaptation only; it does not consume HOT3D hand/object/MANO/contact annotations as predictions.

2026-06-25T13:20:03+08:00 Workbench item-7 style ablation: scored existing raw-fisheye HaWoR outputs on the same HOT3D 3D MANO camera-coordinate metric, without projection review because pinhole projection review is invalid on raw fisheye images. Raw reports: `/data2/ego_annotation_outputs/v19_benchmarks/hot3d_clips/v19_inputs/clip-001849/evaluation/hot3d_hawor_mano3d_raw_fisheye_eval.json`, same for 001850/001851. Raw aggregate: `/data2/ego_annotation_outputs/v19_benchmarks/hot3d_clips/evaluation/hot3d_hawor_mano3d_raw_fisheye_eval_aggregate.json`. Comparison artifact: `/data2/ego_annotation_outputs/v19_benchmarks/hot3d_clips/evaluation/hot3d_hawor_mano3d_raw_vs_pinhole_comparison.json`. Prediction before scoring: if raw fisheye distortion/camera-model mismatch is a systematic input error for HaWoR MANO, pinhole inputs should reduce absolute camera-frame wrist/centroid/joint errors; wrist-subtracted errors may improve less if articulation remains model-limited. Observation: raw-fisheye aggregate medians were wrist 78.93 mm, MPJPE 71.66 mm, wrist-subtracted MPJPE 29.70 mm, vertex-centroid 70.04 mm. Pinhole medians were wrist 25.81 mm, MPJPE 32.86 mm, wrist-subtracted 22.95 mm, vertex-centroid 28.53 mm. Relative reductions from raw: wrist 67.3%, MPJPE 54.1%, wrist-subtracted 22.7%, centroid 59.3%. Same-frame rows raw/pinhole: 871/861; infilled rows raw/pinhole: 29/39. Interpretation: pinhole adaptation reduces a systematic camera/input-model error in absolute 3D hand placement, but residual wrist-subtracted/side-specific errors remain dominated by hand-pose/articulation/infill failure rather than camera model alone. Scope remains fixed-slice HOT3D-Clips hand/MANO state only, not contact/object/occlusion.

2026-06-25T14:11:35+08:00 User corrected item-7 ablation target: raw-fisheye vs pinhole was only a camera-adapter support ablation, not the requested MANO-correction comparison. Mechanism: prediction-side low-support/occlusion interval detection from HaWoR same-frame support, detector score/area, hand-pose magnitude, and temporal continuity; no HOT3D GT consumed by repair. Rejected v1/v3-style broad full-surface interpolation because repair reports showed overrepair of non-target spans. Targeted v4 full interpolation repaired only clip-001850 left frames 62-115 and clip-001851 left frames 86-129; it improved target wrist-subtracted errors but moved wrist/root enough to harm MPJPE in clip-001851. Scored with the existing HOT3D 3D MANO evaluator. Aggregate baseline -> v5 on fixed three-clip slice: overall MPJPE 32.86 -> 32.57 mm; left MPJPE 42.15 -> 40.56 mm; left vertex-centroid 37.43 -> 35.73 mm; wrist and right-hand medians unchanged; left wrist-subtracted median essentially unchanged (34.61 -> 34.69 mm). Target intervals: clip-001850 left 62-115 MPJPE 106.2 -> 94.5 mm and wrist-subtracted 81.9 -> 60.7 mm; clip-001851 left 86-129 MPJPE 70.5 -> 70.7 mm and wrist-subtracted 47.0 -> 43.6 mm. Comparison artifact: `/data2/ego_annotation_outputs/v19_benchmarks/hot3d_clips/evaluation/hot3d_hawor_mano3d_support_temporal_repair_comparison.json`; v5 aggregate: `/data2/ego_annotation_outputs/v19_benchmarks/hot3d_clips/evaluation/hot3d_hawor_mano3d_left_support_temporal_repair_v5_eval_aggregate.json`. Interpretation: wrist-relative temporal articulation repair is a weak positive component and proves the correct ablation target, but it is not an accepted MANO correction. Temporal continuity alone cannot infer occluded hand articulation strongly enough; next mechanism must use stronger prediction-side hand-owned visible-surface/mask-depth evidence, then compare against HaWoR baseline in the same way.

Median joint MPJPE deltas are `clip-001850 -0.001136926 m`, `clip-001849 +0.000851875 m`, and `clip-001851 -0.000999961 m`. Median root-aligned MPJPE deltas are `clip-001850 +0.000304392 m`, `clip-001849 -0.001110242 m`, and `clip-001851 +0.000852540 m`. Interpretation: support gating is a global wrist/root safety mechanism; it is not a monotonic MANO-accuracy mechanism.
- Item-7 causal diagnostic over the three P18 states: parsed 900 hand/frame rows across the three frozen runs. In every row, `visible_surface_depth_order_initial.finite_inside_count=0` and `visible_surface_depth_order_selected_vertex_count=0`. This rules out the explanation that support vertices exist but are discarded by ranking/capping. It localizes the zero-support mechanism to the exact visible-object-mask/depth overlap construction: MANO vertices do not project into finite visible object depth under the ownership/visible mask used by P18. Active object masks and contact-patch priors still exist: `clip-001850` has contact-patch vertices in 150/300 rows, `clip-001849` in 211/300 rows, and `clip-001851` in 241/300 rows. Therefore broad contact priors can pull latent raw translation even when selected visible-surface support is absent.
- Physical interpretation: for keyboard contact, exact visible-object-mask overlap is likely too narrow because hand-owned pixels occlude the touched keys. Physically useful support may be adjacent or latent behind the hand, not visible under projected MANO vertices. This motivates a general nearby/latent support mechanism if the next ablation rejects latent-translation contamination.
- Code intervention for Workbench item 7: added optional solver flag `--freeze-translation-without-visible-surface-support` to `scripts/solve_v18_joint_mano_interval_trajectory.py`. Mechanism: when a row's selected visible-surface depth-order support count is at or below `--translation-gate-min-visible-surface-depth-vertices`, global MANO translation is zeroed inside `hypothesis()` and the translation prior/smoothness/ray prior/bound terms use the effective gated translation. The state records `optimizer_translation_support_gate` and `latent_optimizer_translation_world_m`. ## 2026-06-27T10:28:00+08:00 — item-7 wrist-relative articulation selector analysis
- Workbench position: item 7. After rejecting stricter translation and local-support global translation, the next causal question was whether prediction-side physical features can select rows where wrist-relative interval articulation should be emitted versus reverted to runtime HaWoR.
- Prediction before analysis: if a general selector is viable, physically sane prediction-side features such as same-frame HaWoR support, low visible-joint shift, low raw optimizer shift, low contact-patch distance, stable local support posterior, or low solver residual should distinguish rows where support-gated interval articulation improves HOT3D MANO metrics. If all such features have near-random or inverted discrimination, a selector would be post-hoc metric fitting rather than a physical mechanism.
- Analysis output: `/mnt/truenas-user-home/yiwen/ego_annotation_outputs/v19_benchmarks/hot3d_clips/evaluation/autoresearch/articulation_selector_v1/v19_hot3d_articulation_selector_report.json`, SHA256 `49e2bac856c78221663a37fed75142c255603476059118d43423c3dbdfce23e0`, over 900 matched rows.
- Baseline observation: across all rows, current support-gated interval articulation is weakly positive in aggregate median metrics: all-baseline joint MPJPE median `0.038484086 m`, all-interval `0.038191644 m`; all-baseline root-aligned MPJPE median `0.022522073 m`, all-interval `0.022324546 m`. Row improvement fractions are only `0.4256` for joint MPJPE and `0.43` for root-aligned MPJPE, so the benefit is not rowwise monotonic.
- Feature discrimination: expected-direction AUCs for joint improvement were non-discriminating or inverted: same-frame HaWoR `0.474`, support posterior `0.507`, stable local support `0.470`, visible joint shift max `0.416`, raw optimizer translation norm `0.416`, output-gate shift norm `0.417`, contact initial distance `0.384`, hand pose delta norm `0.407`. The only feature above `0.55` was row patch spread median (`0.591`) and it did not predict root-aligned improvement (`0.491`), so it is not a reliable physical selector.
- Predeclared fixed selector rules did not beat the current all-interval output. `image_safe_visible_shift_max_le_8px` used 375 rows and had joint median `0.038485408 m`; `stable_local_support_only` used 146 rows and had joint median `0.038484086 m`; `image_safe_and_stable_support` used 85 rows and had joint median `0.038484086 m`; `same_frame_and_image_safe` used 362 rows and had joint median `0.038485408 m`. All lose the weak aggregate median benefit of the current support-gated interval state.
- Interpretation: prediction-side summaries available in P18 do not justify a hard wrist-relative articulation selector. The current all-row wrist-relative articulation should remain an uncertainty-carrying hypothesis, not be promoted to accepted MANO correction. The next causal mechanism must add new hand evidence, not another gate over the same contact/visibility summaries.

## Objective
// State the overall goal, final deliverable, and what done means.

Egocentric loco-manipulation video annotation. Need to demonstrate accuracy/physical consistency improvement over existing open-source baseline.

## Workbench
// Maintain the short-term steering state: current status, selected next tasks, observed failures, and open blockers.

## Context
// List the code, docs, resources, and prior evidence the agent must consult or may optionally use.

- Raw data: /data2/egoscale_demo_30h/
- MANO pkl available somewhere over /data/dex_home/
- Compute resources: Local: 3080 (can run relatively light workload). For heavy compute, prioritize using these servers available through wireguard:
  192.168.11.220 A800
  192.168.9.220 4090
  (both username are yiwen; need to look for free GPU by yourself)
  use tmux on servers!
- OpenAI API keys:
  - General purpose: use OPENAI_API_KEY in .env, not other keys you source from other env files; this is billed at standard API price; use with caution.
  - Used for responses API and agent harness (e.g., pi-agent): use OCC_BASEURL and OCC_API_KEY in .env; this is much lower than standard API price.

## Task specifications

### Constitution of work

1. Things to annotate: 1) head camera pose; 2) hand pose and keypoints (MANO); 3) semantic captioning of video; 4) manipulated object pose
2. Desired accuracy: about 5mm (though we do not have ground truth for the data).
3. Directions you can improve compared to existing open-source baselines: 1) creative and systematic composition of modules; 2) LLM/agent-aided judgment/decision/tuning in runtime pipeline; 3) awareness of dexterous manipulation priors and hand-object interaction physics.
4. Deliverables: 1) video with overlaying MANO hand annotation and object annotation; 2) 3D animation of head camera, MANO hands, and object, in world coordinate; 3) side-by-side presentation of annotated video and reconstructed 3D, with semantic caption.

### Specific directions I think of

1. Handling occlusion / missing hand: use human motion prediction prior and Kalman-filter style prediction/update framework.
2. Leveraging LLM: in traditional CV detection pipelines we may need case-by-case human annotation/tuning/modeling. Replace this decision-making process with LLM Agent. Can use pi-coding-agent and harness and OCC_BASEURL and OCC_API_KEY as model provider (use gpt-5.5 there in); consolidate the SOP fed to pi-coding-agent as skill.
3. Physics and contact: it's obviously wrong to pay attention to hand only while treating manipulated objects as part of the "background video". Do mesh-level modeling of the objects, and ensure the process how hands manipulate objects is physically correct. Can annotate contact points as a side benefit. This improves both accuracy and physical plausibility, because physics provide information that vision alone cannot provide.
4. Dexterous manipulation as first-class citizen: ego video is not like gripper-based teleoperation or UMI-collected data: the human has high-DOF hand which can do intricate manipulations. These DOFs are not background noise, but central to our annotation task. Be aware of dexterous manipulation priors such as form closure, force closure and auxiliary contact/support. Can give some local close-up views in reconstructed 3D world view.

### Iterative pipeline design and troubleshooting

(assuming you start from v1)
1. Go through the implementation and results multiple times to ensure v1 specs is fully implemented. No missing planned components. No substituting solution due to "challenge". No taking shortcut. Test on multiple data samples and **inspect results** to ensure every result is uniformly satisfying standard.
2. After v1 is fully closed, reason about the fundamental limit, deep research for improvement directions, and design v2 pipeline.
3. Implement and test v2, iterate as many times as needed and ensure all standards pass, research and reason and design v3, and so forth.

There is no upper limit on the number of versions. You can always keep iterating, according to the guidance of the constitution.

### Quantitative verification

1. Find open-source datasets that look similar to the target dataset, that are paired with ground truth.
2. Test your pipelines of the open-source datasets and measure accuracy against ground truth. Do ablation if relevant.
3. Notice that this accuracy can be used as a partial proof of effectiveness, but minimizing accuracy / overfitting open-source dataset is a nongoal. The real goal is achieving acceptable sanity-check accuracy, pay more attention to physical consistency and dexterous manipulation perspective, and demo the final results.

### Result demo

1. Planned form is side-by-side annotated video + 3D world reconstruction, with real-time text annotations.
2. Pay special attention to the 3D presentation: must be visually impressive to stakeholders; add local close-up of dexterous manipulation where necessary. Must not look like internal diagnostics. The audience must be able to capture the main message immediately.

## Constraints
// Define hard rules the agent must never violate while executing the task.

1. Pipeline planning / version update must interleave with actual implementation.
2.1 One pipeline version must not be prematurely closed without extensive evaluation.
2.2 Every version must have clear upfront pipeline design. No reactive version bumping allowed.
3. Must not substitute planned components with "simpler/easier" ones due to implementation challenge. Any deviation from plan must be grounded in rigorous reasoning, not "operation fact".
4. Must be aware of literature and open-source repos. Do deep research frequently, especially in planning stage. Must not get stuck in local trap knowledge-wise.
5. Must not waste time overfitting accuracy metrics. Pay attention to the physics and the manipulation perspective instead.
6. Must have strong audience awareness in demo/representation. Focus on how to impress audience, not how to dump your operation state into video.

The new observation must either change the next repair target or justify why the artifact has no obvious unlisted fault. This is meant to preserve actual perception and judgment rather than checklist compliance.

Mechanism: the pipeline optimized/accepted a compact observed-support proxy and asymmetric surface proximity, not final 2D silhouette/key-grid/object-pose registration. The two-day object-detection loop removed the old curtain but did not implement the actual object-registration mechanism.
- Hand-object static evidence: for frames 0,60,90,105,120,140,149, `joints_current_v18_world_m` transforms back to `joints_current_v18_camera_m` under `T_world_camera_metric` with numerical-zero median/max error. Therefore the metric world hand separation is not a display-coordinate bug. It is present in the state.
- Frame 140 hand-object distances: left hand nearest-mesh median/min approximately `0.153/0.110 m`; right hand median/min approximately `0.148/0.103 m`. Frame 149 left hand median/min approximately `0.208/0.192 m`. These are physical separations, not uncertainty labels.
- Contact/solver mechanism evidence: P17 factor rows contain `contact_anchor_blockers=[no_persistent_object_frame_contact_anchor, raw_depth_at_hand_pixels_quarantined, agent_interval_judgment_not_a_persistent_object_frame_anchor]` and `contact_anchor_residual_allowed=false`. P18 interval summaries record `output_translation_gate_selected_support_count` median/max `0.0` for both hands, while `output_translation_gate_applied_count=150`. Per-frame examples show raw optimizer translations are then cancelled by the output gate: at frame 140 left raw translation norm about `0.025 m`, right about `0.049 m`, but final `optimized_translation_world_m` is ~`1e-8 m`. Mechanism: the hand solver did not create an attractive contact/near-contact anchor and then reverted global translations because no visible-surface support passed the gate, leaving HaWoR/MANO detector positions far from the object.
- Scoped conclusion: `supportrepair_v2` produced a better mask/compactness proxy, but the final P19b/P19c artifact still fails the named annotation goal. Metrics/P20/P21/autoresearch remain blocked. No implementation was changed in this analysis.

2026-06-29T07:35:00+08:00 — Refined static mechanism after user objections: TRELLIS visible-part blame corrected; raw hand/depth conflict localized
- User objection 1: blaming TRELLIS completion for the wrong mesh is implausible because SAM segmentation is good and TRELLIS should not ruin the visible part.
- Refined object evidence: split projected mesh by face label at frames 120/140/149 using the same P19 renderer projection math. At frame 140, completed-mesh `observed_depth_surface` label alone registers much better to the object-owned mask: mask bbox `[747,257,928,528]`, predicted bbox `[747,245,939,539]`, IoU `~0.874`, extra predicted pixels `5739`, missed mask pixels `198`. The full completed mesh has IoU only `~0.653`, extra predicted pixels `21675`. The `unsupported_uncertain` label alone has almost no overlap at frame 140 (IoU `~0.00035`, extra predicted pixels `15417`) but is rendered as part of the green object body. TRELLIS inferred hidden faces add further extra area (IoU `~0.111`, extra `7763`). At frame 149, even observed-depth-surface projection over-extends (IoU `~0.611`, extra `18642`), indicating pose/depth/edge support problems late, but the visible part is still substantially better than full-body rendering.
- Corrected object mechanism: SAM/object-owned support did improve visible support. The immediate final-artifact failure is that the renderer/state accepts all completed mesh labels, including `unsupported_uncertain` and hidden TRELLIS faces, as one green object body, while the pose/completion objective never enforces final silhouette/key-grid registration. Therefore the claim “TRELLIS ruined the visible part” is too coarse; the visible observed surface is closer to correct, and the final render is wrong because unsupported/hidden/uncertain surfaces are promoted visually as accepted body and because late pose/depth support is not visually registered.
- User objection 2: the hand-object gap is wrong even at raw measurement state because hand and keyboard depth information exist.
- Raw depth evidence: frame 140 keyboard object-owned mask UniDepth median `0.45898 m` (p10 `0.4043`, p90 `0.5308`). Frame 140 left hand HaWoR MANO joint-z median `0.3616 m`; UniDepth sampled at projected left MANO vertices median `0.4158 m`. Frame 140 right hand MANO joint-z median `0.3397 m`; UniDepth at projected right vertices median `0.3862 m`. Thus raw streams already put hands substantially in front of the keyboard depth, with HaWoR farther forward than UniDepth. Frame 120 shows the same pattern more strongly: keyboard median `0.6016 m`, left/right MANO z medians `0.411/0.440 m`, depth at projected hand vertices medians `0.425/0.486 m`.
- Corrected hand mechanism: the final hand-object metric gap is not introduced by renderer world coordinates. It is a measurement-conflict propagation failure. The pipeline used HaWoR MANO as the hand metric state and UniDepth/SAM as object surface, found weak/blocked contact evidence, treated depth/contact terms mostly as one-sided compatibility or nonpenetration/depth-order constraints, and then output-gated away raw optimizer translations because no selected visible-surface support survived. Therefore the raw hand-depth/object-depth disagreement remained in the state and became the visible 10–20 cm metric gap.

2026-06-29T13:58:00+08:00 — Remembered mistake: unsupported Poisson fill was promoted to object body
- User asked why `unsupported_uncertain` Poisson faces were considered part of the object at all. Mechanism: `build_v18_compact_rigid_trellis_completion.py` labels Poisson observed-mesh faces as `observed_depth_surface` if their face centers are near sampled observed surfels, otherwise `unsupported_uncertain`; then it concatenates the entire observed Poisson mesh with kept TRELLIS faces into the completed mesh. The renderer consumed mesh geometry, not the label acceptance semantics, so unsupported Poisson fill was rendered as accepted object body.
- Durable lesson: an uncertainty label is not an uncertainty mechanism unless downstream state/render uses it. Untrusted Poisson fill faces must be excluded from canonical accepted object geometry or rendered as a separate uncertainty hypothesis, never silently promoted into the green object body.

## 2026-06-30T01:42:02+08:00 — V19 clip001851 repaired MANO mask-depth adapter evidence

Prediction before intervention: if the apparent zero hand-mask support on rejected frames was caused by the V19 mask-depth adapter hardcoding a 960x540/0.5 projection target, then replacing the hardcoded target with actual mask-shape/source-size scaling should recover large filtered hand masks on square HOT3D frames without loosening support gates. If no masks were recovered, the failure would remain true visual occlusion or raw hand-mask absence.

Observation: `scripts/build_v19_mano_mask_depth_refit_inputs.py` had a hardcoded `read_mask_960()` resize to `(540,960)` and `projection_support_mask()` target `(540,960)` with `scale=[0.5,0.5]`. HOT3D clip001851 raw/object/hand masks are square 960x960 while UniDepth/source intrinsics are 1408x1408, so the adapter projected and filtered support in the wrong aspect ratio.

Intervention: patched the adapter to infer target mask shape from actual hand/object/raw images, scale projection by `(target_width/source_width, target_height/source_height)`, resize object/hand masks only to that target shape, and record `mask_image_size` plus `source_to_mask_scale_xy` in the legacy annotations. Synced and py-compiled the patched script into `/mnt/user-home/yiwen/ego_annotation_runtime/v19_bundle_a800`.

Observation after repair: reran the builder on frames 95-149 to `$RUN/measurements/hand_candidates/mask_depth_repaired_shape_subset_v1` with no object track subtraction and source size 1408x1408. Counts changed from the prior projection-only report (`left_visible_filtered_masks=0`, `right_visible_filtered_masks=5`) to `left_visible_filtered_masks=55`, `right_visible_filtered_masks=55`. Representative recovered support areas: frame 95 left 11360 px/right 11236 px; 106 left 11947/right 11180; 120 left 17146/right 3957; 140 left 17155/right 21893; 149 left 16058/right 15731.
